reproduce §44a's length-12 table before length 14 is read.

**Checks.**
* **M0 (method).** The length-12 table of §44a is reproduced exactly.
* **M1 (primary).** At length 14, entangled pairs over all remainders: **pass** if
  `C(O) ≤ 0.01 · C(collinear)` with `C(collinear) > 0`; **fail** otherwise.
* **M2 (reported).** The ratio's trend over lengths 10, 12, 14; which remainder classes keep `S(O) = 0` exactly.

**Stated prior.** The breaking at length 12 came only from remainders with spatial pairs on a loop's normal. At
length 14 there are more such remainders (three spatial pairs are possible), so the non-zero orthogonal classes should
gain weight. From 0 at length 10 to 0.00975 at length 12, I expect the ratio to keep rising: **M1 fails**, with the
ratio somewhere between 0.01 and 0.05. Moderate confidence. Classes with only gauge twists, or with one spatial pair
and the rest gauge, should still cancel exactly, as they did at length 12.
* If M1 fails, the cancellation is exact only for two electrons with little else in the closure, and its strength
  falls with the depth of the closure. That is a definite trend, recorded as such, and the rule "entanglement cancels
  orthogonal spin relations" holds only at low depth.
* If M1 passes, the cancellation is stable over three depths.

Script: `logical_bang_census.py --depth 14` (generalising `--depth12`).

### 45a. Result

Run by [`logical_bang_census.py --depth 14`](logical_bang_census.py) (exact integers, about 10 s). **M0 passes:**
§44a's length-12 table is reproduced exactly.

**M1 FAILS, as the prior said:** at length 14 the entangled ratio is `C(O)/C(collinear) = 0.0230`, above 0.01 and
inside the 0.01–0.05 range the prior gave.

| length | `C(collinear)` | `C(O)` | `S(O)` | ratio |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 10 | 0.03268 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| 12 | 0.01626 | 0.000158 | −172 032 | 0.0097 |
| 14 | 0.00739 | 0.000170 | +34 111 488 | 0.0230 |

**Two things the trend shows (M2).**
* **The orthogonal residue stays at about the same size; the collinear signal halves with each step.** `C(O)` goes
  from 0.000158 to 0.000170, while `C(collinear)` falls by about half per two twists. So the ratio rises because the
  coherence of the collinear pairs is diluted by the larger closures, not because the orthogonal pairs stop cancelling.
* **Which remainders keep the exact zero.** At length 14, five of ten orthogonal classes still have `S = 0` exactly:
  only gauge twists, gauge plus one pair on a loop's normal, gauge plus one shared-axis pair, gauge plus two shared
  pairs, and three shared pairs. Over lengths 10–14 the rule is the same: **the cancellation is exact unless the
  remainder has two or more pairs on the loops' normals, or a normal pair together with a shared-axis pair.** Every
  class that breaks it has `C ≤ 0.0011`.

**What this settles.** As registered, the rule "entanglement cancels orthogonal spin relations" holds only at low
depth: exactly to length 10, within 1% at length 12, and not within 1% at length 14. The exact part is a definite
statement about which third bodies preserve the cancellation: it survives any amount of gauge structure and any
amount of structure along the axis the two electrons share, and it is broken only by structure along their spin axes.

**Jim's reading (post hoc, not tested).** Every qubit is potentially orthogonal, and the order of the initial
entanglements may decide whether a closure forms. In this census the order is in the interleaving, and the sign it
gives is what cancels or survives, so the census is already a count over entanglement orders. A test of the reading
would ask which orders close (survive the signed sum) and whether that ordering structure carries a measurable number.
Jim suggests the fine-structure residual (`+0.036`) as the place to look. That needs its own pre-registration.

## 46. Pre-registered: does the order of entanglement close the α residual?

*Fixed in the commit that adds this section, before the script below is written or run. Jim chose to fix the
mapping to α before any signed census of prime closures was computed.*

Jim's reading (§45a): every qubit is potentially orthogonal, and the order of the initial entanglements decides
whether a closure forms. This section turns that into one number, `α⁻¹`, through a mapping fixed here.

### Two structural facts, used to fix the mapping

* **The α census cannot see order.** Its counts, `C(2n,n)` (total) and `2·Catalan(n−1)` (irreducible), are words
  on one axis ([Alpha_Residual.md](Alpha_Residual.md) §1). Twists on one axis commute, so every order of the same
  twists folds to the same sign. Order cancels nothing there.
* **Order cancels only inside irreducible closures.** A composite closure is a sequence of irreducible ones (first
  return), and its fold is the product of their folds. So the signed census keeps the resummation `G = 1/(1 − I)`,
  and order can only reduce `I`. This agrees with §44a: separate electron pairs keep `C = 1`, and only entangled
  (interleaved) ones cancel. The composite weight `w` of Alpha_Residual §2 is not where order acts.

### The mapping, fixed now

* **Alphabet.** All 8 twists with the current `PAULI_MAP` (the same alphabet that gives `α_bare = 2⁻⁷`). A word of
  length `2n` is a closure when it is count-balanced (it then folds to `±I`, §43). It is **prime** when no proper
  prefix is count-balanced.
* **Order interference.** For each multiset `M` of twists, sum the fold signs of its prime orders:
  `S(M) = Σ sign`. The surviving prime amplitude at order `n` is `K_n = Σ_M |S(M)|`, over the `P_n` prime words of
  length `2n`. The **prime survival fraction** is `f_n = K_n / P_n`.
* **To α.** The one-axis irreducible coefficient becomes `2·Catalan(n−1) · f_n`. The total follows from
  `G = 1/(1 − I)`, every composite counted (no `w`). Then `α⁻¹ = 137 + Σ_{n≥2} g_n (1/128)^{n−1}`, as in
  `censusTail`. With every `f_n = 1` this reproduces the cap `137.048130` (checked).

### Checks

* **Q0 (method).** The balanced-word counts 8, 168, 5 120, 190 120 at lengths 2–8 (§43) are reproduced. A sample of
  prime words is checked against `twist_core.pauli_fold`. With `f_n = 1` the cap `137.048130` comes back.
* **Q1 (primary).** **Pass** if `|α⁻¹ − 137.035999| < 0.0040`, that is, if the order mapping comes closer to CODATA
  than the existing structural prediction `w = ½` (`137.032002`, off by `0.0040`). **Fail** otherwise.
* **Q2 (reported).** `f_n` for every order computed, the global signed fraction `|Σ_M S(M)| / P_n` next to it, and
  the equivalent `w` of the result.

### Stated prior: not blind

While fixing the mapping, the order that carries about 97% of the residual was worked out by hand. At length 4 there
are 104 prime words:
* the 8 same-axis primes `x x x̄ x̄`, `x̄ x̄ x x` all fold `+I`;
* the 48 primes on a spatial axis and the gauge axis all fold `+I` (the gauge twists are `±I` and commute);
* the 48 primes on two spatial axes cancel exactly: the 24 interleaved ones (the electron loops `a b ā b̄`) fold
  `−I`, and the 24 nested ones (`a b b̄ ā`) fold `+I`.

So `f_2 = 56/104 = 7/13`. Whatever the higher `f_n` are (each lies between 0 and 1), `α⁻¹` then lies between
`137.04043` (`f_n = 0` for `n ≥ 3`) and `137.04069` (`f_n = 1`). **Q1 fails**, missing CODATA by about `+0.0045`,
slightly worse than `w = ½`. The registration therefore cannot discover the outcome. What it fixes is the method,
and the run checks the hand count and pins the exact value. Recorded anyway: it is the first α value in which the
fold sign, rather than a chosen weight, moves the census off its total. The order mapping removes about 64% of the
total census's overshoot (`0.0481 → 0.0404`, against `0.0360` measured).

Script: `entanglement_order_alpha.py` (to be written after this commit).

### 46a. Result

Run by [`entanglement_order_alpha.py`](entanglement_order_alpha.py) (exact integers, about 2 s to order 7). **Q0
passes:** the balanced-word counts 8, 168, 5 120, 190 120 come back, 3 000 sampled prime words agree with
`pauli_fold`, and `f_n = 1` returns the cap `137.048130`. The prime counts `P_n` also match the independent
first-closure census of [`alpha_residual_bridge.py`](alpha_residual_bridge.py) (8, 104, 2 944, 108 136, 4 525 888).

**Q1 FAILS, as the prior said:** `α⁻¹ = 137.040493`, off CODATA by `+0.004494`. The structural `w = ½` is off by
`−0.003997`, so the order mapping does not come closer.

| `n` | prime words `P_n` | surviving `K_n` | `f_n` |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 8 | 8 | 1 |
| 2 | 104 | 56 | 7/13 = 0.538 |
| 3 | 2 944 | 736 | 1/4 |
| 4 | 108 136 | 12 136 | 0.1122 |
| 5 | 4 525 888 | 227 968 | 0.0504 |
| 6 | 204 981 888 | 4 663 296 | 0.0228 |
| 7 | 9 792 786 432 | 101 279 232 | 0.0103 |

**What the run adds (Q2).**
* **The hand count holds:** `f_2 = 7/13`. The cancelling primes at length 4 are exactly the two-spatial-axis ones,
  where the interleaved electron loops and the nested words have opposite folds.
* **Per-multiset and global cancellation agree at every order.** At each length the multisets that do not cancel to
  zero all have the same sign, so `Σ_M |S(M)| = |Σ_M S(M)|`. The choice between the two readings in §46 does no work.
* **The surviving fraction about halves per order** (ratios 0.54, 0.46, 0.45, 0.45, 0.45, 0.45). The prime closures
  lose coherence steadily with length, as the collinear electron pairs did in §45a.
* **The equivalent `w` is 0.763**, against 0.624 measured and the structural 0.5. The order mapping removes 63% of
  the total census's overshoot.

**What this settles.** Entanglement order, read as fold-sign interference among the orders of each prime closure,
gives a parameter-free value, `α⁻¹ = 137.0405`. No weight is chosen anywhere in it. It sits inside the proven
bracket, below the total census and above CODATA, and it does not close the residual. It is one way the census
reaches the residual, now counted. The open quantity is still the multiplicity between it and the measured value.

**Observation, post hoc and not a test.** The two parameter-free values bracket CODATA from opposite sides at almost
equal distances: `w = ½` gives `−0.0040` and the order mapping gives `+0.0045`. No combination of the two is proposed.
Choosing one after seeing this would be a fit.

## 47. Post hoc: the two readings weighted by their frequency

*A record, not a test. The rule was stated after §46a showed that CODATA lies between the two parameter-free values,
and its value was seen in the same step it was defined.*

Jim: since closures happen every way, the combination is set by the number of ways each happens, and each way has a
specific frequency. In the census a closure's frequency is its cylinder (Kraft) weight `8^(−L)` (`twist_kraft`), so
the unit is each reading's **frequency mass**: the summed `8^(−L)` of the closures that contribute to it.

* **Mode (every listening language, `137.032002`).** Every prime is heard, and each composite in half the languages
  (Alpha_Residual §9n): mass `p + ½(G(p) − p)`, with `p = 0.1932017` the prime mass (Pólya's return probability,
  [Closure_Walk.md](Closure_Walk.md)) and `G(m) = m/(1 − m)` the mass of all closures built from primes of mass `m`.
  `m_A = 0.216334`.
* **Order reading (§46a, `137.040493`).** Only the sign-surviving prime amplitude, every composite counted:
  `q = Σ K_n 8^(−2n) = 0.142514` (to `n = 8`, remaining terms below `5·10⁻⁶`), mass `G(q) = 0.166200`.

The order reading's ways close less often, so its weight is `0.4345`, not ½:

> `α⁻¹ = 137.032002 + 0.4345 × (137.040493 − 137.032002) = 137.035691`, off CODATA by `−0.00031`.

That is about thirteen times closer than either reading alone. It is one way, counted with a weight that is not
chosen, but it is not evidence. Three choices in it could have gone otherwise: Kraft mass as the frequency, the
8-twist alphabet's mass standing for a one-axis α reading, and half the composites as the mode's mass. Only a test
the rule could fail can support it, and §48 is that test.

## 48. Pre-registered: does the frequency weighting transfer to the proton?

*Fixed in the commit that adds this section, before the script below is written or run.*

Alpha_Residual §9o tested the plain ½ rule on the proton and it missed by 51%. This section applies §47's rule,
unchanged, to the same construction.

### The test, fixed now

* **Census π by sector (§9o, unchanged).** The one-axis walk at coupling `4⁻ⁿ`:
  `π_sector = 2 Σ_{n≥0} c_n / (4ⁿ (2n + 1))`, with `c_0 = 1`. The total sector (`c_n = C(2n,n)`) gives `π` exactly.
* **Mode:** `c_n = ½(2·Catalan(n−1) + C(2n,n))`, so `π_mode = (π_irr + π_tot)/2 = 2 + π/4` (closed form, from
  §9o's `π_irr = 4 − π/2`).
* **Order reading:** `c_n = g′_n` from `G′ = 1/(1 − I′)`, `I′ = Σ 2·Catalan(n−1) f_n xⁿ`, with §46a's `f_n` computed
  to `n = 8`. Beyond that, `f_n` is bracketed between 0 and `f_8` (it falls monotonically). Both ends are reported,
  and the verdict must hold at both.
* **Weights:** §47's frequency masses, unchanged (`w_order = 0.4345`). They are substrate frequencies, not refitted
  to the proton.
* **Prediction:** `m_p/m_e = 6 π_rule⁵`, with `π_rule = (1 − w) π_mode + w π_order`, against `1836.15267343`.

**Pass** if `|m_rule / 1836.15267343 − 1| < 10⁻³` (§9o's threshold). **Fail** otherwise.

### Stated prior: fails, and the reason is structural

Both readings give a census π below `π`. The mode gives `2 + π/4 = 2.785`, and the order reading's coefficients
are termwise no larger than `C(2n,n)`, because `f_n ≤ 1` and `G′` increases with `I′`. So
`π_rule ≤ (1 − w)(2 + π/4) + w π ≈ 2.94`, and `m_p/m_e ≤ about 1 320`, at least 28% low. **The rule cannot
transfer under this construction, at any weight short of putting everything on the total sector.** The run gives the
size of the miss. If it fails, §47's agreement stays specific to α at its weak coupling (`1/128`), where the
sectors differ only at the `10⁻²` level. At the proton's `4⁻ⁿ` they differ at order one. The alternative, as in
§9o, is that census π by sector is not the proton's construction.

Script: `frequency_weighted_census.py` (to be written after this commit).

### 48a. Result

Run by [`frequency_weighted_census.py`](frequency_weighted_census.py) (about 7 s). It reproduces §47's numbers
(`w_order = 0.4345`, `α⁻¹ = 137.035691`). As a control, the census-π sum with every `f_n = 1` returns `π` (to
`10⁻⁶` with its asymptotic tail).

**FAILS, as the prior said:** the frequency weighting does not transfer to the proton.

| `f_n` beyond `n = 8` | `π_order` | `π_rule` | `6 π_rule⁵` | off |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | 2.589519 | 2.700295 | 861.40 | −53.1% |
| `f_8` | 2.589696 | 2.700371 | 861.53 | −53.1% |

The bracket on the unknown `f_n` moves the result by `10⁻⁴`, so the verdict does not depend on it. The miss is
larger than the structural bound required (`≤ 1 318`, at least 28% low), because the order reading's π, `2.590`, lies
well below `π`. It is as large as §9o's miss with the plain ½ rule (51%).

**What this settles.** §47's agreement for α (`−0.0003`) does not carry over to the proton under the census-π
construction. Two readings remain, and this test cannot separate them:
* the frequency weighting is right only at α's weak coupling `1/128`, where the readings differ by `10⁻²`;
* census π by sector is not how the proton is built. The total sector alone gives `6π⁵` (off `−1.9·10⁻⁵`), and every
  sector below it moves the ratio by tens of percent.

So §47 stays a post-hoc way for α, counted but unsupported by an independent test. No value is derived and no axiom
is added.

## 49. The frequency rule on the 5-angle proton construction

*Arithmetic, not a test. Nothing in it was uncertain once §48a's `π_rule` was known, so there was nothing to
pre-register.*

Jim asked for the frequency rule on [Proton_Resonance_R_e.md](Proton_Resonance_R_e.md) §6's construction:
`m_p/m_e = |S₃| · π⁵`, five angles (three Jacobi internal coordinates and two chirality-mixing angles, one per
Hermitian-conjugate pair), each contributing `π`.

**The construction carries no census.** Each angle's `π` is a stipulated integration, so the rule has nothing to
weight until an angle's `π` is read from the census. Read through the rule, an angle gives §48a's
`π_rule = 2.700295` in place of `π`. With `k` of the five angles read that way:

| angles read by the rule | `6 π^(5−k) π_rule^k` | off |
|---|---|---|
| 0 (the existing `6π⁵`) | 1836.12 | −1.9·10⁻⁵ |
| 1 | 1578.2 | −14.1% |
| 2 (the chirality-mixing pair) | 1356.5 | −26.1% |
| 3 | 1166.0 | −36.5% |
| 4 | 1002.2 | −45.4% |
| 5 (§48) | 861.4 | −53.1% |

The two chirality-mixing angles are the reading with the most motivation, since they are the ones tied to
Hermitian-conjugate twist pairs, where fold-sign order acts. They put the ratio 26% low. No assignment comes near.

**What this shows.** At the proton's angular scale, the rule replaces a factor of order one (`π_rule/π = 0.860`),
so it cannot be the proton's construction at any angle. At α it acted on a tail suppressed by the coupling `1/128`,
where the readings differ by `10⁻²` and the weighted shift was `0.0037`. A like-for-like proton test needs the same
setting: a weak-coupling census tail on top of `6π⁵`, against the proton's own residual of `−1.9·10⁻⁵`
(`1836.118` vs `1836.153`). [Proton_Resonance_R_e.md](Proton_Resonance_R_e.md) has no such tail, so building one is
the open step. Without it, §47 stays specific to α.

## 50. Pre-registered: α's census tail on the proton

*Fixed in the commit that adds this section, before the script below is written or run. Not blind: see the prior.*

§49 found that the like-for-like setting for the proton is a weak-coupling census tail on top of `6π⁵`, compared
with the proton's own residual. This section builds the one version that adds no choice: **the α tail, unchanged.**

### The test, fixed now

* **Hypothesis.** The census tail at coupling `1/128` is a substrate correction common to every structural leading
  value, so `m_p/m_e = 6π⁵ + T`, where `T` is a reading's α tail with nothing rescaled. Rescaling the tail or using
  another coupling would be a choice, so none is tried.
* **Proton residual:** `δ_p = 1836.15267343 − 6π⁵ = 0.034565` (the measured ratio's uncertainty, `1.1·10⁻⁷`, is
  negligible here).
* **The five readings and their tails `T`:** irreducible `0.015874`, total `0.048130`, mode (`w = ½`) `0.032002`,
  order (§46a) `0.040493`, frequency-weighted (§47) `0.035691`.
* **P1 (primary, like-for-like):** pass if `|T_freq − δ_p| ≤ 0.00031`, the frequency reading's miss on α. **Fail**
  otherwise.
* **P2 (secondary):** the frequency reading is the closest of the five to `δ_p`, as it is for α.

### Stated prior: not blind

Every `T` is known from §46a–§47, and `δ_p` follows from the measured ratio, so the outcome is subtraction:
`T_freq − δ_p = +0.0011`. **P1 fails** (3.3% of the residual, against 0.9% for α) and **P2 passes** (next closest is
the mode, at `−0.0026`). Recorded because the proton's residual, `0.0346`, sits as close to α's (`0.0360`) as the
census readings do to each other. That resemblance is noted here for the first time, and the test asks whether the
reading that fits α best also fits the proton best at the same precision.

Script: `frequency_weighted_census.py --proton-tail` (to be added after this commit).

### 50a. Result

Run by [`frequency_weighted_census.py --proton-tail`](frequency_weighted_census.py) (about 7 s).
`δ_p = 0.034565`; α's residual is `0.035999`.

| reading | tail `T` | `T − δ_p` (proton) | `T − δ_α` (α) | `m_p/m_e = 6π⁵ + T` |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| irreducible | 0.015874 | −0.018691 | −0.020125 | 1836.133983 |
| total | 0.048130 | +0.013565 | +0.012131 | 1836.166239 |
| mode (`w = ½`) | 0.032002 | −0.002563 | −0.003997 | 1836.150111 |
| order (§46a) | 0.040493 | +0.005928 | +0.004494 | 1836.158602 |
| **frequency (§47)** | **0.035691** | **+0.001126** | **−0.000308** | **1836.153800** |

**P1 FAILS, as the prior said.** The frequency reading misses the proton's residual by `+0.0011` (3.3% of it),
against `−0.0003` (0.9%) for α. **P2 PASSES:** it is still the closest of the five, and the order of the readings is
the same for both constants (irreducible and total far off, mode and order on opposite sides, frequency nearest).

**What this says.**
* Under §47's rule, α's tail added to `6π⁵` gives `m_p/m_e = 1836.1538` against `1836.15267`, a relative miss of
  `6·10⁻⁷`, compared with `1.9·10⁻⁵` for `6π⁵` alone. Thirty times closer, with nothing adjusted for the proton.
  None of it is blind: the readings, the rule and both residuals were known before this section.
* The two residuals bracket the frequency tail from opposite sides: α's is `0.00031` above it and the proton's
  `0.00113` below it. A common census tail fits both to about 3%, and does not fit them to α's 0.9%.
* So the resemblance of the two residuals (`0.0346`, `0.0360`) is real at the 4% level. Whether a shared tail is a
  mechanism needs a quantity whose residual has not been seen yet. G's `0.0336` (Gravity.md §4a, recorded there as
  an observation) is the obvious third, but it is already known, so it cannot serve either.

## 2026 update

Two 2026 superconductivity results are read in [Discoveries_2026 §10](Discoveries_2026.md#p10) and
[§12](Discoveries_2026.md#p12): a 5.4% `T_c` rise for six-layer NbSe₂ in a terahertz cavity (with a theory preprint
arguing a passive cavity can only suppress `T_c` under minimal coupling), and pair-density-wave
evidence in UTe₂ (with a bulk X-ray null). Both are open bridges here; no closure census of either has
been computed.
